# Canonical neutral audit intake

Revision: **ESME-CANONICAL-R1-2026-09-06**. Population exported: **105/105**. This document asks questions of an unspecified later subject. It does not evaluate or adopt anything for any repository, framework or sibling lineage.

A legitimate result can be continued maintenance, deliberate non-change, bounded repair, restructuring, migration, replacement, restricted servicing or retirement. “Not triggered”, “a cheaper adequate mechanism already exists”, “insufficient evidence” and “legitimate authority/capability absent” are valid findings. A guard is not optional context to remove from a future checklist.

For retained rows, determine whether the failure is live and whether a proposed mechanism is worth its total cost. For negative or ceremony rows, examine whether the rejected generalisation is being assumed; do not implement it. For unresolved or contested rows, record uncertainty rather than demand conformity. The questions do not presume a standing board, registry, workflow engine, agent or full test stack.

## ESME-C001 — Authorised change intent and obligation revision

**Disposition / examination:** STRONGLY_RETAINED / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** An accepted or executed change does not correspond to a legitimate supported objective, or preservation is silently substituted for an authorised behavioural change.

**Trigger/context:** A request, discovered defect or environmental pressure proposes changing an existing commitment.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** A short accepted issue with concrete examples is enough for a bounded low-risk change; reject or defer an unjustified request.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** An authorised change objective and explicit preserved or revised obligations can be compared with the actual result.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** State the intended difference, affected obligations, legitimate decision maker and acceptance question before endorsing an intervention.

**Required inputs:** conditions: Decision authority and domain meaning must be established; disputed intent cannot be resolved by test count.; related_property_ids: ESME-C038; ESME-C070; interpretation: Related obligations and prerequisites, not a serial scheduling graph. Typed relations in the composition model control the relationship meaning.

**Consumer / authority:** consumer: Those entitled to request or rely on the behaviour, not merely the test author.; decision: An authorised change objective and explicit preserved or revised obligations can be compared with the actual result. / actors: Those entitled to request or rely on the behaviour, not merely the test author.; boundary: Understanding or recommending a change does not itself authorise altering another actor’s obligations. Use actual agreed decision rights and capability; no new role or veto is created by this record.

**Interaction:** exchange: Change request, affected behaviour and requirements history.; purpose: Enable the stated consumer to judge the mechanism, assumptions and postcondition. Use the cheapest adequate channel in NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH; mere publication is not successful communication.

**Omission or retirement:** CANONICAL: Omit a mechanism when its trigger is absent or a cheaper adequate substitute establishes the actual obligation; retire it when its protected obligation or consumer ends. Do not use omission to waive an unchanged supported outcome.; INPUT_SPECIFIC: INPUT_PROPERTY_KEY: A:ESME-001; CONDITIONS: A short accepted issue with concrete examples is enough for a bounded low-risk change; reject or defer an unjustified request. ## ESME-C002 — Decision-relevant, edition-aware maintenance classification

**Disposition / examination:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** A classification label is treated as the whole maintenance task and hides mixed purposes or required work.

**Trigger/context:** Classification affects estimates, authorisation, reporting or selection of maintenance evidence.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** Do not label every commit when the label changes no decision; one explanatory sentence may suffice.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** A mixed change can be described without hiding one purpose or treating a statistic from another taxonomy as comparable.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** Use purpose labels alongside observable activity and artefact scope, permitting multiple purposes; include additive and emergency distinctions when using the 2022 terminology.

## ESME-C003 — Consequential non-code artefact scope

**Disposition / examination:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** A consequential non-code dependency is omitted, leaving source changes insufficient for the actual maintenance obligation.

**Trigger/context:** A maintenance task depends on consequential state outside conventional source code.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** Keep the scope local; no organisation-wide inventory is required when the relevant inputs and consumers are already known.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** The consequential non-code objects and their consumers are in the scope, with irrelevant objects omitted explicitly when disputed.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** Include build, configuration, data, documentation and other non-code objects only where a current or planned maintenance obligation depends on them.

**Required inputs:** A concrete maintenance obligation and enough impact evidence to identify consequential non-code artefacts; no general inventory requirement.

**Consumer / authority:** consumer: Maintainer, operator and downstream consumer of the actual artefact.; decision: Necessary non-code changes and readiness dependencies are included in scope and assigned a consumer. / actors: Maintainer, operator and downstream consumer of the actual artefact.; boundary: Understanding or recommending a change does not itself authorise altering another actor’s obligations. Use actual agreed decision rights and capability; no new role or veto is created by this record.

**Interaction:** exchange: Code, build inputs, schemas, configurations, operational instructions and handover material.; purpose: Enable the stated consumer to judge the mechanism, assumptions and postcondition. Use the cheapest adequate channel in NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH; mere publication is not successful communication.

**Omission or retirement:** CANONICAL: Omit a mechanism when its trigger is absent or a cheaper adequate substitute establishes the actual obligation; retire it when its protected obligation or consumer ends. Do not use omission to waive an unchanged supported outcome.; INPUT_SPECIFIC: INPUT_PROPERTY_KEY: A:ESME-003; CONDITIONS: Exclude irrelevant artefacts explicitly rather than constructing an organisation-wide inventory for a local task. Omit or retire a control when its protected obligation, relevant failure mechanism or decision consumer no longer exists; record unresolved support consequences before removing it.

## ESME-C004 — Demonstrated maintenance readiness and knowledge succession

**Disposition / examination:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** Artefacts are handed over without a capable willing recipient able to perform supported work.

**Trigger/context:** Initial handover, onboarding, departure, concentration or a new support obligation threatens practical maintenance capability.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** One representative diagnosis or change can suffice; stable small projects may instead make support limits or retirement explicit, without inventing extra maintainers.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** The recipient can perform the relevant supported work, or the remaining capability/support limitation is explicit and acted upon.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** At a transfer or continuity risk, establish that an actual willing recipient has the knowledge, access, time and practical ability to perform representative supported work; use explanation, supervised work or equivalent evidence.

**Required inputs:** A real willing recipient, relevant knowledge and artefacts, practical access, time and an opportunity to demonstrate representative supported work. Initial delivery and later turnover are different triggers of the same capability criterion.

**Consumer / authority:** Delivering and receiving maintainers exchange knowledge; managers provide time and authority rather than infer competence from attendance. / Delivering and receiving maintainers exchange knowledge; managers provide time and authority rather than infer competence from attendance.

**Interaction:** recipient_and_boundary: Delivering and receiving maintainers exchange knowledge; managers provide time and authority rather than infer competence from attendance.; content: Use representative tasks, access checks and dialogue to test the receiving maintainer’s ability; preserve rationale and unresolved questions that the task exposes.; adequacy_test: The receiver can complete a representative task or the handoff plan changes to address demonstrated gaps.; non_requirement: No separate person, meeting, registry or tool is required merely because the analytical roles are distinct.

**Omission or retirement:** CANONICAL: Omit a mechanism when its trigger is absent or a cheaper adequate substitute establishes the actual obligation; retire it when its protected obligation or consumer ends. Do not use omission to waive an unchanged supported outcome.; INPUT_SPECIFIC: INPUT_PROPERTY_KEY: A:ESME-003; CONDITIONS: Exclude irrelevant artefacts explicitly rather than constructing an organisation-wide inventory for a local task. Omit or retire a control when its protected obligation, relevant failure mechanism or decision consumer no longer exists; record unresolved support consequences before removing it.; INPUT_PROPERTY_KEY: A:ESME-064; CONDITIONS: A small stable project may need a clear support/retirement policy rather than recruiting multiple maintainers. Omit or retire a control when its protected obligation, relevant failure mechanism or decision consumer no longer exists; record unresolved support consequences before removing it.; INPUT_PROPERTY_KEY: B:ESME-004; CONDITIONS: Compare change with continued useful operation and retirement. End a classification or approval artefact when no consumer uses its distinction, while preserving any actual authorisation requirement.; SOURCE: Frozen B synthesis intake Retirement field; no alteration to original property JSON.; INPUT_PROPERTY_KEY: B:ESME-056; CONDITIONS: Fund sustainable capacity or choose a feasible alternative. Retire operational systems and artefacts when their justified consumers/obligations end, preserving only required historical custody.; SOURCE: Frozen B synthesis intake Retirement field; no alteration to original property JSON.

## ESME-C005 — Identifiable and recoverable maintenance baseline

**Disposition / examination:** STRONGLY_RETAINED / EXAMINED. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** Analysis, evidence or release action refers to the wrong or unreconstructible baseline and environment.

**Trigger/context:** An intervention or investigation requires attributing differences or returning to a known state.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** A versioned file and recorded command can suffice for a local deterministic artefact; retain richer dependencies only where needed.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** Another authorised maintainer can locate the baseline and reproduce the relevant comparison; restoration is separately tested.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** Identify and retain the exact baseline and configuration needed to interpret, build, compare or restore the changed system.

## ESME-C006 — Bounded emergency containment and deliberate degradation

**Disposition / examination:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** A local correction is allowed to propagate beyond the supported scope without a justified containment boundary.

**Trigger/context:** An incident or serious exposure makes ordinary repair timing unsafe and a feasible containment action can reduce harm.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** Use normal repair or deliberate non-change when urgency is absent; a simple reversible feature disablement can need only proportionate evidence.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** The accepted containment objective is achieved within its loss and authority boundary, with recovery or permanent resolution still owned.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** Authorise a bounded temporary change of objective, including disabling or degrading capability when that contains a more consequential failure; state residual obligations, accepted loss, observation and follow-up.

**Required inputs:** conditions: Containment scope, operational capability and recovery or follow-up must be credible.; related_property_ids: ESME-C001; ESME-C049; ESME-C006; interpretation: Related obligations and prerequisites, not a serial scheduling graph. Typed relations in the composition model control the relationship meaning.

**Consumer / authority:** consumer: Affected users and the person authorised to accept temporary degradation.; decision: Temporary degradation and its accepted scope are visible, with a named follow-up decision rather than presumed final correctness. / actors: Affected users and the person authorised to accept temporary degradation.; boundary: Understanding or recommending a change does not itself authorise altering another actor’s obligations. Use actual agreed decision rights and capability; no new role or veto is created by this record.

**Interaction:** exchange: Live service, temporary configuration or patch and follow-up obligation.; purpose: Enable the stated consumer to judge the mechanism, assumptions and postcondition. Use the cheapest adequate channel in NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH; mere publication is not successful communication.

**Omission or retirement:** CANONICAL: Omit a mechanism when its trigger is absent or a cheaper adequate substitute establishes the actual obligation; retire it when its protected obligation or consumer ends. Do not use omission to waive an unchanged supported outcome.; INPUT_SPECIFIC: INPUT_PROPERTY_KEY: A:ESME-005; CONDITIONS: Use normal repair when delay is tolerable; do not invent an emergency fast lane for ordinary convenience. Omit or retire a control when its protected obligation, relevant failure mechanism or decision consumer no longer exists; record unresolved support consequences before removing it.; INPUT_PROPERTY_KEY: A:ESME-089; CONDITIONS: Use a true local correction when feasible; do not invoke containment merely to obtain a passing suite. Omit or retire a control when its protected obligation, relevant failure mechanism or decision consumer no longer exists; record unresolved support consequences before removing it.

## ESME-C007 — Four categories as a universally exhaustive taxonomy

**Disposition / examination:** SUPERSEDED_BY_STRONGER_FORM / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: False.

**Question:** Is the following rejected or superseded inference being assumed, and what bounded alternative addresses the actual need?

**Failure to investigate:** Four familiar purpose labels are treated as an exhaustive disjoint or temporally immutable classification.

**Trigger/context:** An audit or metric system attempts to treat four labels as exhaustive and exclusive.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** Use ESME-C002 and declare the edition and classification purpose.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** Rejection is observable when mixed purposes or additive/emergency dimensions remain representable rather than forced away.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** Candidate proposes requiring every maintenance activity to fit exactly one of four boxes.

## ESME-C008 — Environment-sensitive usefulness feedback

**Disposition / examination:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM / EXAMINED. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** Environment and use change while the maintenance decision continues to assume the old conditions.

**Trigger/context:** Evidence of changed environment, user dissatisfaction or new constraints, not age alone.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** For a stable environment, use occasional review or existing user feedback rather than continuous change.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** An identified change in environmental fit has a reasoned response, which may be no change.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** Observe changes in use, expectations and constraints; decide whether adaptation, servicing, unchanged operation or retirement best preserves justified usefulness.

**Required inputs:** conditions: Environmental observations must be relevant and not just proxy growth metrics.; related_property_ids: ESME-C001; ESME-C081; interpretation: Related obligations and prerequisites, not a serial scheduling graph. Use actual agreed decision rights and capability; no new role or veto is created by this record.

**Interaction:** exchange: System-environment relationship and observed user outcomes.; purpose: Enable the stated consumer to judge the mechanism, assumptions and postcondition. Use the cheapest adequate channel in NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH; mere publication is not successful communication.

**Omission or retirement:** CANONICAL: Omit a mechanism when its trigger is absent or a cheaper adequate substitute establishes the actual obligation; retire it when its protected obligation or consumer ends. Do not use omission to waive an unchanged supported outcome.; INPUT_SPECIFIC: INPUT_PROPERTY_KEY: A:ESME-007; CONDITIONS: For a stable environment, use occasional review or existing user feedback rather than continuous change. ## ESME-C041 — Action-relevant regression-test prioritisation

**Disposition / examination:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** Test ordering fails to improve the consequential detection decision, or a favourable experiment is overgeneralised.

**Trigger/context:** A time-constrained test run benefits from earlier actionable failures.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** Keep the existing order when the suite is cheap or priority estimates are unreliable.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** The ordering objective is stated and omitted tests, if any, remain known.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** Order tests by justified expected information or risk within available time, while separately deciding whether stopping early is acceptable.

## ESME-C049 — Effect-aware recovery, forward repair and changing returnability

**Disposition / examination:** STRONGLY_RETAINED / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** A recovery option is assumed available after irreversible external, data or concurrent effects have changed its feasibility.

**Trigger/context:** A change or exposure can leave consequential state or lose an existing recovery option.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** A pure reversible local edit may need only one verified return path; acceptable reconstruction or forward repair can replace elaborate rollback machinery.

**Criterion and observable evidence:** At the consequential action boundary, the promised remaining recovery/containment route is feasible for the relevant state, or the residual loss is explicitly accepted by the appropriate decision maker.

**Candidate mechanism, not prescribed adoption:** Identify persistent and external effects, demonstrate the restoration, containment or forward-repair route actually promised, and reconsider remaining options before a transition invalidates them; obtain legitimate acceptance for residual irreversibility.

## ESME-C057 — Reachable mixed-version and schema compatibility

**Disposition / examination:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: True.

**Question:** Does the actual subject have this failure and trigger; what adequate evidence or cheaper mechanism would establish the criterion?

**Failure to investigate:** Supported old/new combinations fail while only endpoint versions have been checked.

**Trigger/context:** Rolling upgrades, data conversion or partial migration create old/new participants at the same time.

## ESME-C099 — Human-only maintenance is universally superior

**Disposition / examination:** REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED / EXAMINED. Conditional crosswalk-worthiness: False.

**Question:** Is the following rejected or superseded inference being assumed, and what bounded alternative addresses the actual need?

**Failure to investigate:** Human-only maintenance is asserted to be intrinsically superior regardless of task, tool quality and total cost.

**Trigger/context:** Adjudicate when a negative experiment is used to forbid all maintenance automation.

**Non-trigger or cheaper path:** Use whichever simple human/tool combination is supported for the actual task, including no automation.
